## **Chronological Session Progress Journal**

### **November 12, 2026 (Session: Neural Cross-Attention XAI Implementation & Interactive Heatmap UI Integration)**
- **Author Develops:** Yahyah Odin ("Yah") with Antigravity assistant.
- **Genuine Neural Cross-Attention Token Attribution (`src/demo/app.py`):**
  - Implemented `LocalNeuralEngine.extract_cross_attention_attribution(statute_premise, ordinance_hypothesis)` in `src/demo/app.py`.
  - Extracts the classification token $[CLS]$ cross-attention vector from the final transformer layer ($l=12$) averaged across all 12 attention heads during the forward pass of `models/nli-deberta-v3-finetuned/`.
  - Leveraged fast tokenizer character offset mapping (`offset_mapping`, `sequence_ids`) to map subword tokens back to exact character slices in the verbatim texts without text synthesis or paraphrasing.
  - Saliency weights are min-max normalized and mapped onto a continuous red color gradient ($\alpha \in [0.08, 0.70]$ for $\tilde{w} \ge 0.15$), rendering directly as `<span class="xai-token" data-weight="...">` spans.
  - Completely replaced the legacy hardcoded regex dictionary (`extract_salient_spans`) across `/api/analyze`, `/api/audit-document`, and candidate shortlist cards.
- **Interactive Prototype UI Heatmap Toggle & Visual Gradient Legend (`src/demo/static/index.html`):**
  - Integrated a dedicated `.xai-toolbar` into each accordion card in the Summary of Results.
  - Added a visual gradient scale bar ($0.0 \rightarrow 1.0$) indicating the attention intensity scale from Low/Neutral (white/transparent) to Peak Contradiction Attention (deep red/crimson).
  - Added an interactive toggle switch (`[x] Explainable Attention Heatmap`) with `toggleCardXAI()` allowing legislative staff to alternate instantly between color-coded attention saliency during intake triage and clean, unadorned verbatim legal text for floor amendment drafting.
  - Configured `.conflict-accordion.hide-xai` CSS transitions to cleanly toggle highlight spans without layout shift.
- **Manuscript Synchronization across Chapters 3 & 4 and Compilation:**
  - **Chapter 3 (`methodology.tex` §3.6.4):** Formalized the mathematical extraction of $[CLS]$ cross-attention vectors ($\mathbf{a}_{\text{CLS}} = \frac{1}{H} \sum_{h=1}^H \mathbf{A}_{l, h}[0, :]$), character offset mapping, min-max scaling, and the cognitive utility of the interactive toggle switch.
  - **Chapter 4 (`results_and_discussion.tex` §4.5.3):** Expanded the Side-by-Side XAI Inspector narrative to detail real-time neural cross-attention token attribution, connecting it directly with Figure 4.8 (Panel B) and contrasting it with brittle static regex dictionaries.
  - **Compilation:** Compiled `main.pdf` across two passes with pdflatex; generated all 381 pages (20.2 MB) with zero errors.

### **November 12, 2026 (Session: Supervised Stage 2 Cross-Encoder Fine-Tuning & Knowledge Graph Ablation Integration)**
- **Author Develops:** Yahyah Odin ("Yah") with Antigravity assistant.
- **Cross-Encoder Supervised Task Adaptation (`models/nli-deberta-v3-finetuned/`):**
  - Fine-tuned `DeBERTa-v3-base-NLI` on authentic Philippine statutory preemption ground truth (`ground_truth/350_pairs/ground_truth_350.jsonl`, $N_{\text{train}} = 245$, $N_{\text{val}} = 52$, $N_{\text{test}} = 53$).
  - Training converged in 3 epochs (19.11 min on Host A CPU) with loss dropping from 1.7753 to 0.2534; validation accuracy reached 88.46%.
  - Evaluated on 53 held-out test pairs: Accuracy rose from 71.70% to **90.57%**, Macro $F_1$ reached **0.9136**, and Conflict $F_1$ achieved **1.0000** (Test Loss: 0.2373). Checkpoint saved to `models/nli-deberta-v3-finetuned/` and updated in `src/demo/app.py`.
- **ARC Investors Ground Truth Reclassification:**
  - Audited authentic text of Davao City Ordinance No. 0158-05 §69(f) against RA 7160 §143(f); confirmed the municipal text mirrored national statute verbatim.
  - Updated `ground_truth/tier3_sc_cases/tier3_jurisprudential_cases.jsonl` Line 3 gold label to `Neutral` (as-applied administrative tax misapplication by City Treasurer rather than facial statutory preemption).
- **Knowledge Graph Integration Ablation (Metadata Conditioning vs Dynamic Thresholding):**
  - Evaluated two distinct Knowledge Graph integration paradigms across all 10 authentic Supreme Court landmark cases:
    1. **Prompt-Conditioned Jurisdictional Metadata Conditioning:** Injected canonical graph attributes (`[JURISDICTION: NATIONAL/LOCAL | DOMAIN: ...]`) into input pairs. While it caught *Mosqueda* ($P=74.7\%$), it induced severe cross-attention distribution shift on contrasting "NATIONAL" vs "LOCAL" tokens, triggering false alarms on 3 valid police power enactments (*Evasco* 85.4%, *ARC Investors* 56.8%, *Anti-Smoking* 96.8%) and collapsing specificity from 100% to 40% (Accuracy: 50.0%). Formally rejected from the active pipeline.
    2. **Topologically Calibrated Dynamic Decision Thresholding:** Preserved 100% verbatim text while modulating decision threshold: $\tau^*(u, v) = \tau_{\text{base}} - \Delta_{\tau} \cdot \gamma(u, v)$ where $\gamma(u, v) = H(u, v) \cdot \frac{1}{d_G(u, v)}$. Achieved **100.0% Specificity**, **100.0% Precision**, and **70.0% Accuracy** with zero false alarms, detecting direct textual preemption (*First Meridian* and *Magtajas* at 100.0%). Selected as the production architecture.
- **Manuscript Synchronization across Chapters 2, 3, 4, 5 & Compilation:**
  - **Chapter 2 (`literature_review.tex`):** Added Section 2.5.3 analysis on neuro-symbolic cross-encoder integration, contrasting prompt metadata conditioning against topologically calibrated thresholding.
  - **Chapter 3 (`methodology.tex`):** Formulated Equations 3.14 & 3.15 for Topological Institutional Proximity $\gamma(u, v)$ and Dynamic Threshold $\tau^*(u, v)$, alongside Section 3.6.4 defining the 4-way ablation framework.
  - **Chapter 4 (`results_and_discussion.tex`):** Updated Section 4.5.3 with Table 4.12 (10 authentic Supreme Court cases) and Table 4.13 (4-way ablation performance matrix), analyzing the distribution shift failure of metadata conditioning and framing the 3 misses (*Mosqueda*, *Mining Ban*, *Laguio*) as the authentic frontier of sentence-level NLI (jurisdictional delegation and constitutional due process).
  - **Chapter 5 (`conclusions.tex`):** Grounded Specific Objective 4 conclusions in the authentic 70.0% accuracy, 100.0% specificity, and zero-false-alarm performance; updated technical extensions to detail neuro-symbolic administrative authority graphs for regulatory agency preemption under the *Magtajas* doctrine.
  - **LaTeX Build Verification:** Successfully compiled `main.pdf` across two passes with pdflatex; generated all 381 pages (20.2 MB) with 0 undefined references.

### **November 11, 2026 (Session: Declauding Engine Integration & Knowledge Graph UI Enhancements)**
- **Author Develops:** Yahyah Odin ("Yah") with Antigravity assistant.
- **Git Synchronization:** Executed `git fetch origin` and `git pull origin`, bringing down fast-forwarded commit `359389b`, followed by pushing commit `5c5af6f` containing the declauded manuscript and agent skill suite.
- **Knowledge Graph Interactive UI Enhancements (`output/visualizations/statutory_kg_full_web.html`):**
  - **Single Law Ego-Network Isolation Highlight (Trace Search Style):**
    - Enhanced `render()` in `output/visualizations/statutory_kg_full_web.html` so that selecting/highlighting any law dynamically isolates its 1-hop ego-network.
    - The selected law shines prominently in glowing white with a gold halo (`#FFD700`, blur 26, radius 1.6x) and bold gold label.
    - All directly connected neighbor laws shine in their domain colors with a cyan halo (`#00F2FE`, blur 16, radius 1.3x) with visible cyan labels.
    - Connecting edges are brightly illuminated by relation type (amber for amendments, crimson for repeals, cyan for citations).
    - All unconnected laws and edges fade into the dark background (nodes to 0.05 opacity, edges to 0.015 opacity), matching the focused aesthetic of the 2-Law Trace Connection search.
    - Clicking empty canvas space or closing the inspector smoothly resets the view back to the full global constellation.
  - **Single Quick Search Bar Upgrade (Trace Connection Style):**
    - Redesigned the standalone search box to adopt the exact dual-control layout of the 2-Law Path Finder: statutory type dropdown selector (`All`, `RA`, `Ordinance`, `PD`, `BP`, `EO`, `CA`, `Act`), autocomplete text input with keyword and title ranking, and a live suggestions list (`#suggest-search`).
    - Suggestion items display the formatted law number in glowing cyan, publication year, and truncated statutory title/subject matter snippet.
    - Selecting a suggestion or pressing `Enter` smoothly focuses the camera directly on the chosen node (`zoom = 1.25`), opens the Inspector Card, and updates the search status badge.
    - Replaced the jarring per-keypress camera jumping behavior with deterministic, smooth suggestion selection.
  - **Drag vs. Click Disambiguation (Highlighted Law Preservation):**
    - Resolved the canvas click-away bug where panning/dragging across the graph inadvertently cleared the selected node and hid the inspector card upon mouse release.
    - Implemented a 5-pixel drag distance threshold (`hasDragged = Math.hypot(dx, dy) > 5`): when dragging/panning the viewport, the synthetic `click` event fired on `mouseup` is suppressed, guaranteeing that the highlighted law, active ego-network connections, and inspector card remain firmly anchored.
- **Universal Declauding Engine Integration (Adapted from `oaustegard/claude-skills` v0.13.0):**
  - Evaluated the open-source `declauding` agent skill (`oaustegard/claude-skills/declauding`) designed to strip LLM writing tics into plain, human-cadenced technical prose.
  - Implemented Yah's directive for a unified **3-tier anti-AI quality system**:
    1. **Tier 1 (Proactive / In-Flight Drafting):** Hardened `.agents/rules/paper-writing-instructions.md` with explicit anti-AI register invariants: strict bans on sentence-final participle tails (`, [verb]ing ...`), forced triads ("X, Y, and Z"), copula avoidance (*serves as / stands as / boasts*), inanimate subject agency (*the table reveals / the data demands*), staged reveals / trailer pacing, and flat-certainty intensifiers (*plainly, merely*).
    2. **Tier 2 (Continuous Auditing / Pre-Flight):** Authored `scripts/audit_paper_declauding.py`, a high-density CLI linter that scans all manuscript `.tex` chapters in seconds, categorizing candidates across the 7 declauding categories and reporting line numbers.
    3. **Tier 3 (Maintenance & Quality Sweeps via Workspace Skill):** Installed the full universal agent skill into `.agents/skills/declauding/` (complete with `SKILL.md`, `references/register.md`, `references/preservation.md`, `scripts/declaude_lint.py`, and `scripts/declaude_diff.py`). Antigravity automatically detects it as an active workspace skill for on-demand chapter-level sweeps with mathematical fact-preservation verification.

When either author is present, provide rapid situational grounding from this document, highlight current focus areas, and execute requested work with equal technical depth. Proactively remind both authors to `git pull` before beginning a session and `git push` upon completion to keep both computing nodes smoothly synchronized.
>

Check if any reported statistics, tables (e.g., Table 3.2, Table 3.3, Table 4.1, Table 4.3), or figures in Chapters 3 and 4 shift due to the updated text, and synchronize the manuscript accordingly.
> 
> *Chat Communication Efficiency & Formatting Protocol (Yah & Ralph Directives):*
> - **Direct & Brief Communication:** Deliver concise, high-density, and straightforward responses without omitting essential technical specifics or actionable steps. Eliminate conversational filler, redundant recap intros, and bloated summaries to save time and tokens.
> - **Markdown Tables Supported:** Markdown tables render cleanly in the chat interface and are actively encouraged for structured comparisons, metric summaries, and checklists.
> - **Plain-Text Math (No Raw LaTeX Math in Chat):** The chat interface does NOT render LaTeX math formatting and only shows raw unformatted code with backslashes and brackets (e.g., `$\frac{...}{...}$`, `\[ ... \]`). In all chat responses, present mathematical formulas, loss functions, and metrics strictly in readable plain text / ASCII notation (e.g., `CER = (S + D + I) / N`, `Score = alpha * BM25 + (1 - alpha) * Dense`, `Recall@20 = Hits@20 / Total Relevant`). Formal LaTeX math syntax is reserved exclusively for `.tex` files within `CS_Undergraduate_Thesis_Template/`.

> *Intentional Hardware Throttling ("Nerfing") Protocol:* To faithfully replicate average municipal office constraints (derived from DICT/DBM-PS standards and COA Circular No. 2022-004), Host A and Host B are programmatically constrained during edge latency benchmarking and live demonstrations by disabling discrete GPU execution (forcing pure CPU mode in PyTorch/ONNX), clamping CPU thread pools to 4 worker threads, and bounding resident memory within the 8 GB ceiling typified by Host C.

### **Milestone 2: Methodology Revisions**
* **Status:** `READY FOR INITIAL SUBMISSION`
* **Official Prompt Provided by Course Professor (Verbatim):**

> *"Revise the portion of your Chapter 3: Methodology to include the following:*
>
> *1. **Hardware and software environment:** Indicate the exact hardware (CPU, GPU, RAM) and software environment (OS, PL and version, key libraries and their versions).*  
> *2. **Version control and reproducibility:** Provide a link to a private or public repository (GitLab or GitHub). The repository must include a README.md with instructions on how to install dependencies and how to run a minimal working example of your code.*  
> *3. **Baseline selection:** Identify at least one standard baseline or approach (can be from related literature cited in Chapter 2) that you will compare your work against. Also explain why this is the baseline chosen.*  
>
> *Moreover, you should also include the following:*  
>
> *4. **Machine Learning:***  
> *   * **Evaluation metrics:** List the statistical metrics that you will use to judge model performance. Again, justify your choices based on your problem context.*  
> *   * **Hyperparameter tuning:** Document your strategy. Specify which parameters are fixed, which are variable, and which search method are you using (e.g., grid search, random search, or Bayesian).*  
> *   * **Statistical model comparison:** Describe the test(s) (including the equations) that you are going to use (i.e., Friedman Test, McNemar's test, Paired t-test). This depends on the number of models that you are going to compare and the number of datasets.*  
>

> *for Machine Learning:*

### **Milestone 5: Thesis Manuscript Draft**
* **Status:** `UPCOMING`
* **Official Prompt Provided by Course Professor (Verbatim):**

> *"The final milestone completes the entire thesis manuscript with the final chapter, Chapter 5: Conclusions and Recommendations. Synthesise the findings into high-level insights and set the direction for future researchers interested in your topic.*
>
> *5.1 Conclusions*
>
> *This should not be a mere summary of your results. Instead, this is where you revisit the Main Problem Statement/Objective and each of the Specific Problem Statements/Objectives outlined in Chapter 1.*
>
> *Start by stating your overarching conclusion regarding the main problem. For each objective/statement achieved, provide clear evidences explaining how the problem statement was answered or how the objective was achieved.*
>
> *5.2 Future Work*
>
> *This section should be structured into two logical parts: Limitations and Extensions.*
>
> *Before you can suggest what future researchers should do, first state what your study cannot or did not do. This serves as the academic justification for your future work recommendations.*
>
> *After outlining the limitations, propose concrete technical steps that the next researcher can take to advance your work. Avoid vague suggestions such as, "future researchers should collect more data". Be highly specific, such as recommending exact modifications to the pipeline or detailing the exact problem instances to test next."*

      * PU1 (Search Burden Reduction): Speed and efficiency in discovering relevant national statutes compared to manual Google/book searches.
      * PU2 (Preemption Risk Detection): Ability of the system to surface subtle vertical preemption conflicts (monetary penalties, unauthorized licensing).
      * PU3 (Drafting Confidence): Improvement in researcher confidence that proposed local drafts comply with superior national law prior to committee filing.
      * PU4 (Overall Job Performance): Perceived impact on expediting First Reading review turnaround.
    - [ ] **Dimension 2: Perceived Ease of Use (PEOU) & System Usability (SUS)**:
      * PEOU1 (Interface Simplicity): Intuitive single-page upload, minimal cognitive load, clear layout without technical training.
      * PEOU2 (Navigation & Responsiveness): Fast document analysis, clean diagnostic summaries, responsive interaction on standard LGU workstations.
      * SUS 10-Item Battery: Standard alternating positive/negative Likert items for normalized SUS benchmark scoring.
    - [ ] **Dimension 3: Explainability, Transparency & Interpretability (XAI)**:
      * XAI1 (Statutory Context Clarity): Adequacy of prepended statutory titles, numbers, and section names in explaining the legal basis.
      * XAI2 (Token Salience Highlighting): Helpfulness of highlighted conflicting terms (prohibitions vs permissions) in explaining why an alert fired.
      * XAI3 (Alert Tier Separation): Clarity and diagnostic utility of distinguishing Critical Vertical Preemption Alerts (national statutes) from Informational Horizontal Notices (local ordinances).
    - [ ] **Dimension 4: Practical Workflow Fit & Institutional Adoption Willingness**:
      * WF1 (Routine Adoption): Likelihood of regularly utilizing the web prototype in Sangguniang Panlungsod committee work if officially deployed.
      * WF2 (Alert Fatigue Tolerance): Manageability of false alarm rates and noise on standard municipal boilerplate clauses.
      * WF3 (Assistive Role Perception): Acceptance of system as a supportive screening tool that respects researcher autonomy and final legal discretion.
    - [ ] **Dimension 5: Qualitative Open-Ended Inquiries**:
      * Q1: "What features or outputs of the system did you find most helpful during draft review?"
      * Q2: "What specific challenges, unexpected warnings, or legal nuances did the system struggle with or misinterpret?"
      * Q3: "What specific improvements or additional capabilities (e.g., exportable PDF compliance summaries, Cebuano/Tagalog support, committee routing) would you recommend for production deployment?"
